The audio track is present throughout the file. Its measured length is **602.624 seconds**. There are no complete one-second intervals below −60 dBFS in the analyzed mono signal. That establishes continuous recorded sound, although it does not mean the sound maintains a constant perceived loudness.

The signal is strongly concentrated at low frequencies. Approximately **98% of the measured energy** in the analysis lies below **1 kHz**, with about **65% between 80 and 300 Hz**. Prominent aggregate spectral components occur near **131, 146, 164, 174, and 195 Hz**. These are properties of the combined soundtrack; they cannot be assigned to an individual voice.

The spectrogram shows persistent low-frequency energy with changing intensity, harmonic structure, and higher-frequency components. Together with the running sound-loop pages, this supports an inference of a **bass-heavy, tonal or droning mixture with fluctuating texture**. That wording is an analytical inference, not a claim that I directly heard its timbre.

An audio classifier frequently categorized portions as music. Other windows received vehicle-like, rumbling, vocal-music, singing, or humming classifications. Such labels are imperfect pattern matches. They do not establish that actual vehicles, animals, or a particular singer are audible.
